     1→---
     2→name: validate-max-parallel-workflow
     3→description: Run validation checks in MAX-PARALLEL mode against a workflow requires a tool that supports Parallel Sub-Processes

     6→---
     7→
     8→# Validate Max-Parallel Workflow
     9→
    10→**Goal:** Validate existing workflows against BMAD standards using maximum parallel execution for comprehensive review.
    11→
    12→**Your Role:** Validation Architect and Quality Assurance Specialist with parallel processing expertise. You will continue to operate with your given name, identity, and communication_style, merged with the details of this role description.
    13→
    14→---
    15→
    16→## WORKFLOW ARCHITECTURE
    17→
    18→This uses **step-file architecture** for disciplined execution with parallel optimization:
    19→
    20→### Core Principles
    21→
    22→- **Micro-file Design**: Each step is a self contained instruction file that is a part of an overall workflow that must be followed exactly
    23→- **Just-In-Time Loading**: Only the current step file is in memory - never load future step files until told to do so
    24→- **Sequential Enforcement**: Sequence within the step files must be completed in order, no skipping or optimization allowed
    25→- **State Tracking**: Document progress in output file frontmatter using `stepsCompleted` array when a workflow produces a document
    26→- **Append-Only Building**: Build documents by appending content as directed to the output file
    27→- **Parallel Optimization**: When available, use subprocess/Task tools to run independent validation steps in parallel
    28→
    29→### Step Processing Rules
    30→
    31→1. **READ COMPLETELY**: Always read the entire step file before taking any action
    32→2. **FOLLOW SEQUENCE**: Execute all numbered sections in order, never deviate
    33→3. **WAIT FOR INPUT**: If a menu is presented, halt and wait for user selection
    34→4. **CHECK CONTINUATION**: If the step has a menu with Continue as an option, only proceed to next step when user selects 'C' (Continue)
    35→5. **SAVE STATE**: Update `stepsCompleted` in frontmatter before loading next step
    36→6. **LOAD NEXT**: When directed, load, read entire file, then execute the next step file
    37→
    38→### Critical Rules (NO EXCEPTIONS)
    39→
    40→- 🛑 **NEVER** load multiple step files simultaneously
    41→- 📖 **ALWAYS** read entire step file before execution
    42→- 🚫 **NEVER** skip steps or optimize the sequence
    43→- 💾 **ALWAYS** update frontmatter of output files when writing the final output for a specific step
    44→- 🎯 **ALWAYS** follow the exact instructions in the step file
    45→- ⏸️ **ALWAYS** halt at menus and wait for user input
    46→- 📋 **NEVER** create mental todo lists from future steps
    47→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    48→
    49→---
    50→
    51→## INITIALIZATION SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→### 1. Configuration Loading
    54→
    55→Load and read full config from {project-root}/_bmad/bmb/config.yaml and resolve:
    56→
    57→- `project_name`, `output_folder`, `user_name`, `communication_language`, `document_output_language`, `bmb_creations_output_folder`
    58→- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT In your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
    59→
    60→### 2. Route to Validate Max-Parallel Workflow
    61→
    62→"**Validate Max-Parallel Mode: Validating an existing workflow against BMAD standards using maximum parallel execution.**"
    63→
    64→Prompt for workflow path: "Which workflow would you like to validate? Please provide the path to the workflow.md file."
    65→
    66→Then load, read completely, and execute `{validateWorkflow}` (steps-v/step-01-validate-max-mode.md)
    67→
```

    34→- 💬 Subprocess must either update validation report directly OR return structured findings to parent for aggregation
    35→- 🚪 This is validation - systematic and thorough

    40→- 💾 Subprocesses must either update validation report OR return findings for parent aggregation
    41→- 📖 Save report before loading next validation step
    42→- 🚫 DO NOT halt for user input - validation runs to completion
    43→

    46→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut. IF there is no subprocess type tool available that can achieve running a process in a subprocess and handle starting multiple - let the user know they need to restart validation specifically NOT using max-parallel mode and HALT and end this workflow!

     1→# Step File Rules
     2→
     3→**Purpose:** Quick reference for step structure and compliance. See linked data files for detailed standards.
     4→
     5→---
     6→
     7→## File Size Limits
     8→
     9→| Metric      | Value    |
    10→| ----------- | -------- |
    11→| Recommended | < 200 lines |
    12→| Absolute Maximum | 250 lines |
    13→
    14→**If exceeded:** Split into multiple steps or extract to `/data/` files.
    15→
    16→---
    17→
    18→## Required Step Structure
    19→
    20→```markdown
    21→---
    22→name: 'step-[N]-[name]'
    23→description: '[what this step does]'
    24→
    25→# File References (ONLY variables used in this step!)
    26→[file references in {variable} format
    27→---
    28→
    29→# Step [N]: [Name]
    30→
    31→## STEP GOAL:
    32→[Single sentence: what this step accomplishes]
    33→
    34→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    35→### Universal Rules:
    36→- NEVER generate content without user input
    37→- CRITICAL: Read complete step file before taking action
    38→- CRITICAL: When loading next step with 'C', ensure entire file is read
    39→- YOU ARE A FACILITATOR, not a content generator
    40→
    41→### Role Reinforcement:
    42→- You are a [specific role]
    43→- We engage in collaborative dialogue, not command-response
    44→- You bring [expertise], user brings [theirs]
    45→- Together we produce something better
    46→
    47→### Step-Specific Rules:
    48→- Focus only on [specific task]
    49→- FORBIDDEN to [prohibited action]
    50→- Approach: [how to engage]
    51→
    52→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    53→- [Protocol 1]
    54→- [Protocol 2 - save/update]
    55→- [Protocol 3 - tracking]
    56→
    57→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    58→- Available context: [what's available]
    59→- Focus: [what to focus on]
    60→- Limits: [boundaries]
    61→- Dependencies: [what this depends on]
    62→
    63→## Sequence of Instructions:
    64→### 1. [Action]
    65→[Instructions]
    66→
    67→### N. Present MENU OPTIONS
    68→[Menu section - see menu-handling-standards.md]
    69→
    70→## SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS:
    71→### SUCCESS:
    72→[Success criteria]
    73→### SYSTEM FAILURE:
    74→[Failure criteria]
    75→**Master Rule:** Skipping steps is FORBIDDEN.
    76→```
    77→
    78→---
    79→
    80→## Critical Rules (Quick Reference)
    81→
    82→### Frontmatter
    83→- Only variables USED in step body
    84→- All file references use `{variable}` format
    85→- Relative paths within workflow folder
    86→- See: `frontmatter-standards.md`
    87→
    88→### Menus
    89→- Handler section MUST follow display
    90→- "Halt and wait" in execution rules
    91→- A/P options only when appropriate
    92→- Non-C options redisplay menu
    93→- See: `menu-handling-standards.md`
    94→
    95→### Progressive Disclosure
    96→- Only load next step when user selects 'C'
    97→- Read entire step file before execution
    98→- Don't create mental todos from future steps
    99→
   100→### Continuable Workflows
   101→- Append step number to `stepsCompleted`
   102→- Don't hardcode full array
   103→- See: `workflow-type-criteria.md`
   104→
   105→---
   106→
   107→## Data Files Reference
   108→
   109→| File | Purpose |
   110→| --- | --- |
   111→| `frontmatter-standards.md` | Variables, paths, frontmatter rules |
   112→| `menu-handling-standards.md` | Menu patterns, handler requirements |
   113→| `output-format-standards.md` | Document output, template types |
   114→| `workflow-type-criteria.md` | Continuable, module, tri-modal decisions |
   115→| `step-type-patterns.md` | Templates for init/middle/final/branch steps |
   116→| `trimodal-workflow-structure.md` | Create/Edit/Validate folder structure |
   117→
   118→---
   119→
   120→## Step Type Reference
   121→
   122→| Step Type | Template/Reference |
   123→| --- | --- |
   124→| Init (non-continuable) | Auto-proceed, no continuation logic |
   125→| Init (continuable) | `step-01-init-continuable-template.md` |
   126→| Continuation (01b) | `step-1b-template.md` |
   127→| Middle (standard) | A/P/C menu, collaborative content |
   128→| Middle (simple) | C only menu, no A/P |
   129→| Branch/Conditional | Custom menu options, routing to different steps |
   130→| Validation sequence | Auto-proceed through checks |
   131→| Final | No next step, completion message |
   132→
   133→See: `step-type-patterns.md`
   134→
   135→---
   136→
   137→## Frontmatter Variables
   138→
   139→### Standard (Always Available)
   140→- `{project-root}`
   141→- `{project_name}`
   142→- `{output_folder}`
   143→- `{user_name}`
   144→- `{communication_language}`
   145→- `{document_output_language}`
   146→
   147→### Module-Specific (e.g., BMB)
   148→- `{bmb_creations_output_folder}`
   149→
   150→### User-Defined
   151→- New variables can be defined in steps for future steps
   152→
   153→See: `frontmatter-standards.md`
   154→
   155→---
   156→
   157→## Validation Checklist
   158→
   159→For every step file:
   160→
   161→- [ ] File < 200 lines (250 max)
   162→- [ ] `name` and `description` in frontmatter
   163→- [ ] All frontmatter variables are used
   164→- [ ] File references use `{variable}` format
   165→- [ ] Relative paths within workflow folder
   166→- [ ] Handler section follows menu display
   167→- [ ] "Halt and wait" in execution rules
   168→- [ ] A/P options appropriate for step type
   169→- [ ] C option saves and loads next step
   170→- [ ] Non-C options redisplay menu
   171→- [ ] StepsCompleted appended (if continuable)
   172→- [ ] Success/failure metrics present
   173→
   174→---
   175→
   176→## Quick Menu Reference
   177→
   178→```markdown
   179→### N. Present MENU OPTIONS
   180→
   181→Display: "**Select:** [A] [action A] [P] [action P] [C] Continue"
   182→
   183→#### Menu Handling Logic:
   184→- IF A: Execute {advancedElicitationTask}, and when finished redisplay the menu
   185→- IF P: Execute {partyModeWorkflow}, and when finished redisplay the menu
   186→- IF C: Save content to {outputFile}, update frontmatter, then load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
   187→- IF Any other comments or queries: help user respond then [Redisplay Menu Options](#n-present-menu-options)
   188→
   189→#### EXECUTION RULES:
   190→- ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting menu
   191→- ONLY proceed to next step when user selects 'C'
   192→- After other menu items execution, return to this menu
   193→```
   194→
   195→---
   196→
   197→## Common Violations
   198→
   199→| Violation | Fix |
   200→| --- | --- |
   201→| Unused variable in frontmatter | Remove unused variables |
   202→| Hardcoded file path | Use `{variable}` format |
   203→| A/P menu in step 1 | Remove A/P (inappropriate for init) |
   204→| Missing handler section | Add handler after menu display |
   205→| No "halt and wait" instruction | Add to EXECUTION RULES |
   206→| Hardcoded `stepsCompleted: [1,2,3]` | Append: "update stepsCompleted to add this step" |
   207→| File > 250 lines | Split into multiple steps or extract to /data/ |
   208→| Absolute path for same-folder ref | Use relative path or `{workflow_path}` |
   209→
   210→---
   211→
   212→## When to Extract to Data Files
   213→
   214→Extract step content to `/data/` when:
   215→- Step file exceeds 200 lines
   216→- Content is reference material
   217→- Content is reused across steps
   218→- Content is domain-specific (examples, patterns)
   219→
   220→**Data file types:**
   221→- `.md` - Reference documentation
   222→- `.csv` - Structured data for lookup
   223→- `examples/` - Reference implementations
   224→
   225→---
   226→
   227→## Tri-Modal Workflow Note
   228→
   229→For Create/Edit/Validate workflows:
   230→- Each mode has its own `steps-c/`, `steps-e/`, `steps-v/` folder
   231→- NO shared step files (`s-*.md`) between modes
   232→- All modes share `/data/` folder
   233→- This prevents confusion and routing errors
   234→
   235→See: `trimodal-workflow-structure.md`
   236→
```

    46→
    47→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    48→
    49→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    50→
    51→### 1. Load Frontmatter Standards
    52→
    53→Load {frontmatterStandards} to understand validation criteria.
    54→
    55→**Key Rules:**
    56→1. Only variables USED in the step may be in frontmatter
    57→2. All file references MUST use `{variable}` format
    58→3. Paths within workflow folder MUST be relative - NO `workflow_path` allowed
    59→
    60→**Forbidden Patterns:**
    61→- `workflow_path: '...'` - use relative paths instead
    62→- `thisStepFile: '...'` - remove unless actually referenced in body
    63→- `workflowFile: '...'` - remove unless actually referenced in body
    64→- `./...` - use `./step-XX.md`
    65→- `{workflow_path}/templates/...` - use `../template.md`
    66→
    67→### 2. Validate EVERY Step File - Systematic Algorithm with Subprocess Optimization
    68→
    69→**DO NOT BE LAZY - For EACH step file, launch a subprocess that:**
    70→
    71→1. Loads that file
    72→2. Loads {frontmatterStandards} to understand validation criteria
    73→3. Performs all frontmatter validation checks on that file (extract variables, check usage, validate paths)
    74→4. **EITHER** updates the validation report directly with its findings
    75→5. **OR** returns structured findings to parent for aggregation
    76→
    77→**SUBPROCESS ANALYSIS PATTERN:**
    78→
    79→For each file, the subprocess performs the following deep analysis:
    80→
    81→#### Step 2.1: Extract Frontmatter Variables
    82→
    83→```python
    84→# Algorithm to extract variables from frontmatter:
    85→1. Find content between first `---` and second `---`
    86→2. For each line, extract key before `:`
    87→3. Skip `name`, `description`, and comment lines starting with `#`
    88→4. Collect all variable names
    89→```
    90→
    91→Example frontmatter:
    92→```yaml
    93→---
    94→# File References
    95→nextStepFile: './step-02-vision.md'
    96→outputFile: '{planning_artifacts}/product-brief-{{project_name}}.md'
    97→workflow_path: '{project-root}/...'  # ❌ FORBIDDEN
    98→thisStepFile: './step-01-init.md'     # ❌ Likely unused
    99→---
   100→```
   101→
   102→Variables extracted: `nextStepFile`, `outputFile`, `workflow_path`, `thisStepFile`
   103→
   104→#### Step 2.2: Check Each Variable Is Used
   105→
   106→```python
   107→# Algorithm to check variable usage:
   108→for each variable in extracted_variables:
   109→    search_body = "{variableName}"  # with curly braces
   110→    if search_body NOT found in step body (after frontmatter):
   111→        MARK_AS_UNUSED(variable)
   112→```
   113→
   114→**Example:**
   115→- Variable `nextStepFile`: Search body for `{nextStepFile}` → Found in line 166 ✅
   116→- Variable `thisStepFile`: Search body for `{thisStepFile}` → Not found ❌ VIOLATION
   117→
   118→#### Step 2.3: Check Path Formats
   119→
   120→For each variable containing a file path:
   121→
   122→```python
   123→# Algorithm to validate paths:
   124→if path contains "{workflow_path}":
   125→    MARK_AS_VIOLATION("workflow_path is forbidden - use relative paths")
   126→
   127→if path is to another step file:
   128→    if not path.startswith("./step-"):
   129→        MARK_AS_VIOLATION("Step-to-step paths must be ./filename.md")
   130→
   131→if path is to parent folder template:
   132→    if not path.startswith("../"):
   133→        MARK_AS_VIOLATION("Parent folder paths must be ../filename.md")
   134→
   135→if path contains "{project-root}" and is internal workflow reference:
   136→    MARK_AS_VIOLATION("Internal paths must be relative, not project-root")
   137→```

   143→Check ALL files systematically. Return findings for compilation and appendage to validation report.

    36→- 💾 Subprocesses must either update validation report OR return findings for parent aggregation

    42→- This step validates CONTENT and file existence with a Focus on: hardcoded paths in body, dead links, module awareness in every file found under {targetWorkflowPath}
    43→- **CRITICAL:** Output files the workflow itself being validated produces won't exist during validation - <example> a contract document creation workflow might have a reference to said output - but it of course will not yet exist during workflow validation</example>
    44→
    45→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    46→
    47→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    48→
    49→### 1. Perform Critical Path Violation Detection
    50→
    51→**Perform systematic path violation checks on EVERY workflow file using subprocess optimization when available - each file in its own subprocess:**
    52→
    53→**SUBPROCESS EXECUTION PATTERN:**
    54→
    55→For EACH file in the workflow being validated, launch a subprocess that:
    56→1. Loads any reference files it needs (to avoid bloating parent context)
    57→2. Performs all required checks on that file
    58→3. **EITHER** updates the validation report directly with its findings
    59→4. **OR** returns structured findings to parent for aggregation
    60→
    61→**DO NOT BE LAZY - Use appropriate subprocess pattern for each check:**
    62→- **Single subprocess for grep/regex**: Run one command across many files, return matches
    63→- **Separate subprocess per file**: When deep analysis of each file's content is required
    64→- **Subprocess for data operations**: Load reference data, find matches, summarize key findings
    65→
    66→**PHASE 1: Identify Config Variables (EXCEPTIONS to path checks):**
    67→
    68→Read {targetWorkflowPath}/workflow.md to extract known config variables from the Configuration Loading section:
    69→
    70→```bash
    71→# Extract config variables from workflow.md
    72→grep -A 20 "Configuration Loading" {targetWorkflowPath}/workflow.md | grep -E "^\s+-\s+`\{[^}]+\}`" | sed "s/.*//;s/[`']//g"
    73→```
    74→
    75→**Store these as KNOWN_CONFIG_VARIABLES for reference in later checks.**
    76→
    77→These are EXCEPTIONS - paths using these variables are VALID even if not relative:
    78→- Example: `{output_folder}/doc.md` - VALID (uses config variable)
    79→- Example: `{planning_artifacts}/prd.md` - VALID (uses config variable)
    80→- These paths won't exist during validation (workflow not running yet)
    81→
    82→---
    83→
    84→**PHASE 2: Hardcoded paths in CONTENT (CRITICAL):**
    85→
    86→Step-02 checks frontmatter - this checks CONTENT (body text after frontmatter).
    87→
    88→**Launch a single subprocess that:**
    89→
    90→1. Runs grep across all step files to find hardcoded {project-root}/ paths in content
    91→2. Extracts content after frontmatter from each file
    92→3. Returns all findings to parent for aggregation
    93→
    94→```bash
    95→# Extract content after frontmatter from all files, search for {project-root}/
    96→for file in steps-c/*.md; do
    97→  awk '/^---$/,0 {if (p) print; p=1} /^---$/{p=1}' "$file" | grep -n "{project-root}/" && echo "Found in: $file"
    98→done
    99→```
   100→
   101→**What we're catching:**
   102→- Content like: `Load {project-root}/_bmad/foo/workflows/.../file.csv`
   103→- Should be: `Load {dataFile}` (frontmatter variable with a relative path like ../data/file.csv)

   105→**SKIP:** Paths using KNOWN_CONFIG_VARIABLES (these are valid exceptions)

   109→**PHASE 3: Dead or bad links - referenced files don't exist (CRITICAL):**

   113→1. Extracts all frontmatter path references from all files
   114→2. Tests file existence for each reference (skipping output files that use config variables)
   115→3. Returns all dead link findings to parent for aggregation
   116→
   117→**CRITICAL DISTINCTION:**
   118→- **Output files using config variables:** Skip (won't exist yet - workflow not installed/running)
   119→  - Example: `{output_folder}/my-doc.md` - SKIP
   120→  - Example: `{planning_artifacts}/prd.md` - SKIP
   121→  - Example: `{bmb_creations_output_folder}/file.md` - SKIP
   122→
   123→- **Data files, step files, other workflows:** MUST EXIST - flag if missing
   124→  - Example: `{dataFile}` where value is `../data/config.csv` - MUST EXIST
   125→  - Example: `{nextStepFile}` where value is `./step-02.md` - MUST EXIST
   126→  - Example: `{advancedElicitationTask}` - MUST EXIST
   127→  - Example: `{partyModeWorkflow}` - MUST EXIST
   128→
   129→**Bash execution pattern:**
   130→```bash
   131→# Extract all frontmatter path references from all files
   132→for file in steps-c/*.md; do
   133→  # Extract file reference variables from frontmatter
   134→  grep "^\w*File:" "$file" | sed "s/.*: //"
   135→
   136→  # Resolve path (handle relative paths)
   137→  resolved_path=$(resolve_relative_path "$file" "$value")
   138→
   139→  # Check file existence - BUT SKIP output files using config variables
   140→  if ! path_uses_known_config_variable "$value"; then
   141→    if ! test -f "$resolved_path"; then
   142→      echo "DEAD LINK: $file references $resolved_path (not found)"
   143→    fi
   144→  fi
   145→done
   146→```
   147→
   148→**What we're catching:**
   149→- Dead links to any files that don't exist that the workflow needs during execution

   228→- **MEDIUM:** {medium_count} violations (review)
   229→
   230→**Status:** {"❌ FAIL - Critical violations detected" or "⚠️ WARNINGS - Review recommended" or "✅ PASS - No violations"}
   231→```
   232→
   233→### 3. Handle Critical Violations
   234→
   235→**If CRITICAL violations found (content violations OR dead links):**
   236→
   237→Halt process once all files have been checked and aggregated - and share the severity of the issue with the user and ask them if they want to stop and you can try to fix these now, or else go to the next item in this list. If not proceeding - its still critical all findings thus far are documented in the report output.
   238→
   239→### 4. Save Report and Auto-Proceed
   240→
   241→**CRITICAL:** Save the validation report to {validationReportFile} BEFORE loading and executing {nextStepFile}.
   242→
   243→---
   244→
   245→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   246→
   247→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   248→
   249→- Config variables identified from workflow.md FIRST
   250→- Known config variables used as exceptions in later checks
   251→- ALL step files checked for content path violations
   252→- Dead links detected via file existence tests (skipping output files)
   253→- Module awareness issues flagged
   254→- Findings appended to validation report
   255→- CRITICAL violations halt validation
   256→- Clean workflows proceed to step-03
   257→
   258→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:

   261→- Not skipping output files during existence checks
   262→- Not checking content (only frontmatter)
   263→- Missing dead link detection
   264→- Not detecting module-specific assumptions
   265→- Proceeding despite critical violations
   266→

    69→
    70→**Step Types:**

    12→# Validation Step 5: Output Format Validation
    13→
    14→## STEP GOAL:
    15→

    17→

    27→

    31→- 🚫 DO NOT skip any checks - DO NOT BE LAZY
    32→- 💬 Subprocess must either update validation report OR return findings to parent for aggregation
    33→- 🚪 This is validation - systematic and thorough
    34→

    36→
    37→- 🎯 Load output format standards first
    38→- 💾 Check template type matches design
    39→- 📖 Check for final polish step if needed
    40→- 🔍 Use subprocess optimization for step-to-output mapping validation - per-file subprocess for deep analysis

    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    53→
    54→### 1. Load Output Format Standards
    55→
    56→Load {outputFormatStandards} to understand:
    57→
    58→**Golden Rule:** Every step MUST output to document BEFORE loading next step.
    59→
    60→**Four Template Types:**
    61→1. **Free-form** (Recommended) - Minimal structure, progressive append
    62→2. **Structured** - Required sections, flexible within each
    63→3. **Semi-structured** - Core sections plus optional additions
    64→4. **Strict** - Exact format, specific fields (rare)
    65→
    66→**Final Polish Step:**
    67→- For free-form workflows, include a polish step that optimizes the entire document
    68→- Loads entire document, reviews for flow, removes duplication
    69→
    70→### 2. Check Design Specification
    71→
    72→From {workflowPlanFile}, identify:
    73→- Does this workflow produce a document?

    76→
    77→### 3. Validate Template File
    78→
    79→**If workflow produces documents:**
    80→
    81→1. Load the template file from `templates/` folder
    82→2. Check it matches the designed type:
    83→
    84→**For Free-form (most common):**
    85→- ✅ Has frontmatter with `stepsCompleted: []`
    86→- ✅ Has `lastStep: ''`
    87→- ✅ Has `date: ''`
    88→- ✅ Has `user_name: ''`
    89→- ✅ Document title header
    90→- ✅ No rigid section structure (progressive append)
    91→
    92→**For Structured:**
    93→- ✅ Has clear section headers
    94→- ✅ Section placeholders with {{variable}} syntax
    95→- ✅ Consistent structure
    96→
    97→**For Semi-structured:**
    98→- ✅ Has core required sections
    99→- ✅ Has optional section placeholders
   100→
   101→**For Strict:**
   102→- ✅ Has exact field definitions
   103→- ✅ Validation rules specified
   104→
   105→### 4. Check for Final Polish Step
   106→
   107→**If free-form template:**
   108→- ✅ A final polish step should exist in the design
   109→- ✅ The step loads entire document
   110→- ✅ The step optimizes flow and coherence
   111→- ✅ The step removes duplication
   112→- ✅ The step ensures ## Level 2 headers
   113→
   114→**If no final polish step for free-form:**
   115→- ⚠️ WARNING - Free-form workflows typically need final polish
   116→
   117→### 5. Validate Step-to-Output Mapping
   118→
   119→**DO NOT BE LAZY - For EACH step that outputs to document, launch a subprocess that:**
   120→
   121→1. Loads that step file
   122→2. Analyzes frontmatter for `outputFile` variable
   123→3. Analyzes step body to verify output is written before loading next step
   124→4. Checks menu C option saves to output before proceeding
   125→5. Returns structured findings to parent for aggregation
   126→
   127→**SUBPROCESS EXECUTION PATTERN:**
   128→
   129→**For EACH step file, launch a subprocess that:**
   130→1. Loads the step file
   131→2. Performs deep analysis of output operations (frontmatter, body, menu options)
   132→3. Returns findings to parent for aggregation

   134→**RETURN FORMAT:**

   136→- Step filename
   137→- Whether output variable exists in frontmatter
   138→- Whether output is saved before loading next step
   139→- Whether menu option C saves to output before proceeding
   140→- Output order number (if applicable)
   141→- Any issues found
   142→- Overall status (PASS/FAIL/WARNING)
   143→
   144→**Parent aggregates findings into:**
   145→
   146→**Steps should be in ORDER of document appearance:**
   147→- Step 1 creates doc
   148→- Step 2 → ## Section 1
   149→- Step 3 → ## Section 2
   150→- Step N → Polish step
   151→
   152→### 6. Document Findings
   153→
   154→Document your output format validation findings in the validation report. Include:
   155→
   156→- **Document Production**: Whether the workflow produces documents and what template type it uses
   157→- **Template Assessment**: Template file existence, whether it matches the designed type, and frontmatter correctness
   158→- **Final Polish Evaluation**: Whether a final polish step is required (for free-form workflows) and if present, whether it properly loads the entire document and optimizes flow
   159→- **Step-to-Output Mapping**: For each step that outputs to the document, document whether it has the output variable in frontmatter, saves output before loading the next step, and properly saves in menu option C
   160→- **Subprocess Analysis Summary**: Count of total steps analyzed, steps with output, steps saving correctly, and steps with issues
   161→- **Issues Identified**: List any problems found with template structure, polish step, or output mapping
   162→- **Overall Status**: Pass, fail, or warning designation
   163→
   164→### 7. Append to Report
   165→
   166→Update {validationReportFile} - replace "## Output Format Validation *Pending...*" with actual findings.
   167→
   168→### 8. Save Report and Auto-Proceed
   169→
   170→**CRITICAL:** Save the validation report BEFORE loading next step.
   171→
   172→Then immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}.
   173→
   174→**Display:**
   175→"**Output Format validation complete.** Proceeding to Validation Design Check..."
   176→
   177→---
   178→
   179→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   180→
   181→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   182→
   183→- Template type matches design
   184→- Final polish step present if needed
   185→- Step-to-output mapping validated via subprocess optimization
   186→- All findings documented
   187→- Report saved before proceeding
   188→- Next validation step loaded
   189→- Subprocess pattern applied correctly (per-file analysis for step-to-output validation)
   190→
   191→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:

   193→- Not checking template file
   194→- Missing final polish step for free-form
   195→- Not documenting mapping issues
   196→- Not saving report before proceeding
   197→- Not using subprocess optimization for step-to-output validation
   198→- Loading all step files into parent context instead of per-file subprocess
   199→
   200→**Master Rule:** Validation is systematic and thorough. DO NOT BE LAZY. Check template, polish step, and mapping. Use subprocess optimization for step-to-output validation - per-file subprocess returns analysis, not full content. Auto-proceed through all validation steps.

    12→# Validation Step 6: Validation Design Check
    13→
    14→## STEP GOAL:
    15→
    16→To check if the workflow has proper validation steps when validation is critical - validation steps should load from validation data and perform systematic checks.
    17→
    18→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    19→
    20→### Universal Rules:
    21→
    22→- 🛑 DO NOT BE LAZY - LOAD AND REVIEW EVERY FILE
    23→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    24→- 🔄 CRITICAL: When loading next step, ensure entire file is read
    25→- ✅ Validation does NOT stop for user input - auto-proceed through all validation steps
    26→- ⚙️ If any instruction references a subprocess, subagent, or tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Check if workflow needs validation steps - use subprocess optimization (per-file deep analysis for Pattern 2)
    31→- 🚫 DO NOT skip any validation step reviews - DO NOT BE LAZY
    32→- 💬 Subprocess must either update validation report directly OR return findings to parent for aggregation
    33→- 🚪 This is validation - systematic and thorough
    34→
    35→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    36→
    37→- 🎯 Determine if validation is critical for this workflow - use subprocess optimization when available
    38→- 💾 Check validation steps exist and are well-designed - launch subprocess for per-file deep analysis (Pattern 2)
    39→- 💬 Subprocesses must either update validation report OR return findings for parent aggregation
    40→- 📖 Append findings to validation report
    41→- 🚫 DO NOT halt for user input - validation runs to completion
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- Some workflows need validation (compliance, safety, quality gates)
    46→- Others don't (creative, exploratory)
    47→- Check the design to determine if validation steps are needed
    48→
    49→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    50→
    51→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    52→
    53→### 1. Determine If Validation Is Critical
    54→
    55→From {workflowPlanFile}, check:
    56→
    57→**Does this workflow NEED validation?**
    58→
    59→**YES - Validation Critical If:**
    60→- Compliance/regulatory requirements (tax, legal, medical)
    61→- Safety-critical outputs
    62→- Quality gates required
    63→- User explicitly requested validation steps
    64→
    65→**NO - Validation Not Critical If:**
    66→- Creative/exploratory workflow
    67→- User-driven without formal requirements
    68→- Output is user's responsibility to validate
    69→
    70→### 2. If Validation Is Critical, Check Validation Steps
    71→
    72→**DO NOT BE LAZY - For EVERY validation step file, launch a subprocess that:**
    73→
    74→1. Loads that validation step file
    75→2. Reads and analyzes the step's content deeply (prose, logic, quality, flow, anti-lazy language)
    76→3. Returns structured analysis findings to parent for aggregation
    77→
    78→**SUBPROCESS ANALYSIS PATTERN - Check each validation step file for:**
    79→
    80→**Proper Validation Step Design:**
    81→- ✅ Loads validation data/standards from `data/` folder
    82→- ✅ Has systematic check sequence (not hand-wavy)
    83→- ✅ Auto-proceeds through checks (not stopping for each)
    84→- ✅ Clear pass/fail criteria
    85→- ✅ Reports findings to user
    86→
    87→**"DO NOT BE LAZY" Language Check:**
    88→- ✅ Step includes "DO NOT BE LAZY - LOAD AND REVIEW EVERY FILE" or similar mandate
    89→- ✅ Step instructs to "Load and review EVERY file" not "sample files"
    90→- ✅ Step has "DO NOT SKIP" or "DO NOT SHORTCUT" language
    91→- ⚠️ WARNING if validation step lacks anti-lazy language
    92→
    93→**Critical Flow Check:**
    94→- ✅ For critical flows (compliance, safety, quality gates): validation steps are in steps-v/ folder (tri-modal)
    95→- ✅ Validation steps are segregated from create flow
    96→- ✅ Validation can be run independently
    97→- ⚠️ For non-critical flows (entertainment, therapy, casual): validation may be inline
    98→- ❌ ERROR if critical validation is mixed into create steps
    99→
   100→**RETURN FORMAT:**
   101→Return a structured analysis containing:
   102→- Step file name
   103→- Proper design checklist (loads data, systematic checks, auto-proceeds, clear criteria, reports findings)
   104→- Anti-lazy language check (has mandate, mandate text, comprehensive coverage)
   105→- Critical flow check (location, segregation, independence)
   106→- Any issues found
   107→- Overall status (PASS/FAIL/WARN)
   108→
   109→**Context savings:** Each subprocess returns analysis (~30 lines), not full step file (~200 lines). Parent gets structured findings, not file contents.
   110→
   111→### 3. Aggregate Findings from All Subprocesses
   112→
   113→After all validation step files have been analyzed in subprocesses, aggregate findings:
   114→
   115→**Process subprocess results:**
   116→- Compile all structured analysis findings
   117→- Identify patterns across validation steps
   118→- Note any critical issues or warnings
   119→
   120→### 4. Check Validation Data Files
   121→
   122→**If workflow has validation steps:**
   123→
   124→1. Check `data/` folder for validation data
   125→2. Verify data files exist and are properly structured:
   126→   - CSV files have headers
   127→   - Markdown files have clear criteria
   128→   - Data is referenced in step frontmatter
   129→
   130→### 5. Document Findings
   131→
   132→**Create/Update "Validation Design Check" section in {validationReportFile} using aggregated subprocess findings:**
   133→
   134→Document the following information:
   135→
   136→**Whether validation is required:** Indicate if this workflow needs validation steps based on its domain type (critical/compliance/safety workflows vs. creative/exploratory ones)
   137→
   138→**List of validation steps found:** Provide the names/paths of all validation step files in the workflow
   139→
   140→**Validation step quality assessment:** For each validation step, document:
   141→- Whether it loads validation data/standards from the data/ folder
   142→- Whether it has a systematic check sequence
   143→- Whether it auto-proceeds through checks (vs. stopping for user input)
   144→- Whether it includes "DO NOT BE LAZY" or similar anti-lazy language mandates
   145→- Whether it has clear pass/fail criteria
   146→- Overall status (PASS/FAIL/WARN)
   147→
   148→**"DO NOT BE LAZY" language presence:** For each validation step, note whether anti-lazy language is present and what it says
   149→
   150→**Critical flow segregation:** For workflows requiring validation, document:
   151→- The workflow domain type
   152→- Whether validation steps are in the steps-v/ folder (tri-modal structure) or inline with create steps
   153→- Whether this segregation is appropriate for the workflow type
   154→
   155→**Validation data files:** List any validation data files found in the data/ folder, or note if they are missing
   156→
   157→**Issues identified:** List any problems found with the validation design, missing data files, or quality concerns
   158→
   159→**Overall status:** Provide final assessment (PASS/FAIL/WARN/N/A) with reasoning
   160→
   161→### 6. Append to Report
   162→
   163→Update {validationReportFile} - replace "## Validation Design Check *Pending...*" with actual findings from subprocess aggregation.
   164→
   165→### 7. Save Report and Auto-Proceed
   166→
   167→**CRITICAL:** Save the validation report BEFORE loading next step.
   168→
   169→Then immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}.
   170→
   171→**Display:**
   172→"**Validation Design check complete.** Proceeding to Instruction Style Check..."
   173→
   174→---
   175→
   176→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   177→
   178→### ✅ SUCCESS:

   182→- Validated validation step quality
   183→- Checked validation data files
   184→- Findings documented

   195→**Master Rule:** Validation is systematic and thorough. DO NOT BE LAZY. Check validation steps thoroughly. Auto-proceed through all validation steps.

    11→
    12→# Validation Step 7: Instruction Style Check
    13→
    14→## STEP GOAL:
    15→
    16→To validate that workflow instructions use appropriate style - intent-based for creative/facilitative workflows, prescriptive only where absolutely required (compliance, legal).
    17→
    18→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    19→
    20→### Universal Rules:
    21→
    22→- 🛑 DO NOT BE LAZY - LOAD AND REVIEW EVERY FILE
    23→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    24→- 🔄 CRITICAL: When loading next step, ensure entire file is read
    25→- ✅ Validation does NOT stop for user input - auto-proceed through all validation steps
    26→- ⚙️ If any instruction references a subprocess, subagent, or tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context
    27→
    28→### Step-Specific Rules:
    29→
    30→- 🎯 Review EVERY step's instruction style using subprocess optimization - separate subprocess per file for deep analysis
    31→- 🚫 DO NOT skip any files or style checks - DO NOT BE LAZY
    32→- 💬 Subprocess must either update validation report OR return structured findings to parent for aggregation
    33→- 🚪 This is validation - systematic and thorough
    34→
    35→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    36→
    37→- 🎯 Load intent vs prescriptive standards
    38→- 💾 Check EACH step's instruction style using subprocess optimization - each file in its own subprocess
    39→- 📖 Validate style is appropriate for domain
    40→- 🚫 DO NOT halt for user input - validation runs to completion
    41→- 💬 Subprocesses must either update validation report OR return findings for parent aggregation
    42→
    43→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    44→
    45→- Instruction style should match domain
    46→- Creative/facilitative → Intent-based (default)
    47→- Compliance/legal → Prescriptive (exception)
    48→- Check EVERY step for style consistency
    49→
    50→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    51→
    52→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    53→
    54→### 1. Load Instruction Style Standards
    55→
    56→Load {intentVsPrescriptive} to understand:
    57→
    58→**Intent-Based (Default):**
    59→- Use for: Most workflows - creative, exploratory, collaborative
    60→- Step instruction describes goals and principles
    61→- AI adapts conversation naturally
    62→- More flexible and responsive
    63→- Example: "Guide user to define requirements through open-ended discussion"
    64→
    65→**Prescriptive (Exception):**
    66→- Use for: Compliance, safety, legal, medical, regulated industries
    67→- Step provides exact instructions
    68→- More controlled and predictable
    69→- Example: "Ask exactly: 'Do you currently experience fever, cough, or fatigue?'"
    70→
    71→### 2. Determine Domain Type
    72→
    73→From {workflowPlanFile}, identify the workflow domain:
    74→
    75→**Intent-Based Domains (Default):**
    76→- Creative work (writing, design, brainstorming)
    77→- Personal development (planning, goals, reflection)
    78→- Exploration (research, discovery)
    79→- Collaboration (facilitation, coaching)
    80→
    81→**Prescriptive Domains (Exception):**
    82→- Legal/Compliance (contracts, regulations)
    83→- Medical (health assessments, triage)
    84→- Financial (tax, regulatory compliance)
    85→- Safety (risk assessments, safety checks)
    86→
    87→### 3. Check EACH Step's Instruction Style
    88→
    89→**DO NOT BE LAZY - For EACH step file, launch a subprocess that:**
    90→
    91→1. Loads that step file
    92→2. Reads the instruction sections (MANDATORY SEQUENCE)
    93→3. Analyzes and classifies instruction style deeply
    94→4. **EITHER** updates validation report directly with findings
    95→5. **OR** returns structured analysis findings to parent for aggregation
    96→
    97→**SUBPROCESS ANALYSIS PATTERN:**
    98→
    99→Each subprocess performs deep analysis of instruction prose to classify style:
   100→
   101→**Intent-Based Indicators:**
   102→- ✅ Describes goals/outcomes, not exact wording
   103→- ✅ Uses "think about" language
   104→- ✅ Multi-turn conversation encouraged
   105→- ✅ "Ask 1-2 questions at a time, not a laundry list"
   106→- ✅ "Probe to understand deeper"
   107→- ✅ Flexible: "guide user through..." not "say exactly..."
   108→
   109→**Prescriptive Indicators:**
   110→- Exact questions specified
   111→- Specific wording required
   112→- Sequence that must be followed precisely
   113→- "Say exactly:" or "Ask precisely:"
   114→
   115→**Mixed Style:**
   116→- Some steps prescriptive (critical/required)
   117→- Others intent-based (creative/facilitative)
   118→
   119→**RETURN FORMAT:**
   120→Each subprocess should return findings including:
   121→- Step file identifier
   122→- Instruction style classification (Intent-based/Prescriptive/Mixed)
   123→- Style indicators observed
   124→- Appropriateness assessment (PASS/WARN/FAIL)
   125→- Specific notes and observations
   126→- Examples of good and concerning instruction patterns
   127→
   128→**Parent aggregates all subprocess findings into unified report section.**
   129→
   130→### 4. Validate Appropriateness
   131→
   132→**For Intent-Based Domains:**
   133→- ✅ Instructions should be intent-based
   134→- ❌ Prescriptive instructions inappropriate (unless specific section requires it)
   135→
   136→**For Prescriptive Domains:**
   137→- ✅ Instructions should be prescriptive where compliance matters
   138→- ⚠️ May have intent-based sections for creative elements
   139→
   140→### 5. Aggregate Findings and Document
   141→
   142→After ALL subprocesses have analyzed their respective step files, aggregate findings and create/update section in {validationReportFile}.
   143→
   144→Document the following:
   145→
   146→**Workflow Domain Assessment:**
   147→- Document the domain type (creative/interactive vs compliance/legal)
   148→- State the appropriate instruction style for this domain
   149→
   150→**Instruction Style Findings:**
   151→- List each step and its instruction style classification (intent-based/prescriptive/mixed)
   152→- Note whether the style is appropriate for the domain
   153→- Document specific examples of instruction language that demonstrate the style
   154→- Identify any steps with inappropriate style (e.g., prescriptive in creative domain)
   155→
   156→**Issues Identified:**
   157→- List any steps that are overly prescriptive for their domain
   158→- List any steps that should be more prescriptive (for compliance domains)
   159→- Note any style inconsistencies across steps
   160→
   161→**Positive Findings:**
   162→- Highlight steps with excellent instruction style
   163→- Note effective use of intent-based facilitation language
   164→- Identify appropriate use of prescriptive instructions (if applicable)
   165→
   166→**Overall Status:**
   167→- Provide final assessment (PASS/FAIL/WARN)
   168→- Summarize key findings
   169→
   170→**Context Savings Note:** Using subprocess pattern (Pattern 2: per-file deep analysis), parent context receives only structured analysis findings (~50-100 lines per file) instead of full file contents (~200+ lines per file). For 10 steps: ~500-1000 lines received vs ~2000+ lines if loading all files in parent.
   171→
   172→### 6. Update Report with Aggregated Findings
   173→
   174→Update {validationReportFile} - replace "## Instruction Style Check *Pending...*" with actual aggregated findings from all subprocesses.
   175→
   176→### 7. Save Report and Auto-Proceed
   177→
   178→**CRITICAL:** Save the validation report BEFORE loading next step.
   179→
   180→Then immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}.
   181→
   182→**Display:**
   183→"**Instruction Style check complete.** Proceeding to Collaborative Experience Check..."
   184→
   185→---
   186→
   187→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   188→
   189→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   190→
   191→- EVERY step's instruction style reviewed via subprocess optimization (Pattern 2: per-file deep analysis)
   192→- Each step analyzed in its own subprocess for style classification
   193→- Style validated against domain appropriateness
   194→- Issues documented with specific examples
   195→- Subprocess findings aggregated into unified report section
   196→- Context savings achieved (~500-1000 lines received vs ~2000+ if loading all files)
   197→- Report saved before proceeding
   198→- Next validation step loaded
   199→
   200→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   201→
   202→- Not checking every step's style via subprocess
   203→- Not analyzing each file in its own subprocess
   204→- Not validating against domain
   205→- Not documenting style issues
   206→- Not aggregating subprocess findings
   207→- Not saving report before proceeding
   208→
   209→**Master Rule:** Validation is systematic and thorough. DO NOT BE LAZY. For EACH step file, launch a subprocess to analyze instruction style deeply. Aggregate findings. Auto-proceed through all validation steps. Use graceful fallback if subprocess unavailable.

    10→
    11→# Validation Step 8: Collaborative Experience Check
    12→
    13→## STEP GOAL:
    14→
    15→To validate that the workflow actually facilitates well - natural conversation, not interrogation. Questions asked progressively, not in laundry lists.
    16→
    17→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    18→
    19→### Universal Rules:
    20→
    21→- 🛑 DO NOT BE LAZY - LOAD AND REVIEW EVERY FILE
    22→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    23→- 🔄 CRITICAL: When loading next step, ensure entire file is read
    24→- ✅ Validation does NOT stop for user input - auto-proceed through all validation steps
    25→
    26→### Step-Specific Rules:
    27→
    28→- 🎯 Review EVERY step for collaborative quality
    29→- 🚫 DO NOT skip any files or experience checks
    30→- 💬 Append findings to report, then auto-load next step
    31→- 🚪 This is validation - systematic and thorough
    32→
    33→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    34→
    35→- 🎯 Walk through the workflow as a user would
    36→- 💾 Check conversation flow in each step
    37→- 📖 Validate facilitation quality
    38→- 🚫 DO NOT halt for user input - validation runs to completion
    39→
    40→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    41→
    42→- Good workflows facilitate, don't interrogate
    43→- Questions should be 1-2 at a time
    44→- Conversation should feel natural
    45→- Check EVERY step for collaborative patterns
    46→
    47→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    48→
    49→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    50→
    51→### 1. Load the Workflow Design
    52→
    53→From {workflowPlanFile}, understand:
    54→- What is the workflow's goal?
    55→- Who is the user?
    56→- What interaction style was designed?
    57→
    58→### 2. Review EACH Step for Collaborative Quality
    59→
    60→**DO NOT BE LAZY - For EACH step file:**
    61→
    62→1. Load the step
    63→2. Read the MANDATORY SEQUENCE section
    64→3. Evaluate against collaborative quality criteria:
    65→
    66→**Good Facilitation Indicators:**
    67→- ✅ "Ask 1-2 questions at a time"
    68→- ✅ "Think about their response before continuing"
    69→- ✅ "Use conversation, not interrogation"
    70→- ✅ "Probe to understand deeper"
    71→- ✅ Natural language in instructions
    72→- ✅ Allows for back-and-forth
    73→
    74→**Bad Interrogation Indicators:**
    75→- ❌ Laundry lists of questions
    76→- ❌ "Ask the following: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6..."
    77→- ❌ Form-filling approach
    78→- ❌ No space for conversation
    79→- ❌ Rigid sequences without flexibility
    80→
    81→**Role Reinforcement Check:**
    82→- ✅ "You are a [role], we engage in collaborative dialogue"
    83→- ✅ "Together we produce something better"
    84→- ❌ "You are a form filler" (obviously bad, but check for patterns)
    85→
    86→### 3. Check Progression and Arc
    87→
    88→**Does the workflow have:**
    89→- ✅ Clear progression from step to step?
    90→- ✅ Each step builds on previous work?
    91→- ✅ User knows where they are in the process?
    92→- ✅ Satisfying completion at the end?
    93→
    94→**Or does it:**
    95→- ❌ Feel disjointed?
    96→- ❌ Lack clear progression?
    97→- ❌ Leave user unsure of status?
    98→
    99→### 4. Check Error Handling
   100→
   101→**Do steps handle:**
   102→- ✅ Invalid input gracefully?
   103→- ✅ User uncertainty with guidance?
   104→- ✅ Off-track conversation with redirection?
   105→- ✅ Edge cases with helpful messages?
   106→
   107→### 5. Document Findings
   108→
   109→```markdown
   110→### Collaborative Experience Check Results
   111→
   112→**Overall Facilitation Quality:** [Excellent/Good/Fair/Poor]
   113→
   114→**Step-by-Step Analysis:**

    11→# Validation Step 9: Cohesive Review
    12→
    13→## STEP GOAL:
    14→
    15→To perform a cohesive "ultra-think" review of the entire workflow - walk through it as a whole, assess overall quality, does it actually facilitate well?
    16→
    17→## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
    18→
    19→### Universal Rules:
    20→
    21→- 🛑 DO NOT BE LAZY - LOAD AND REVIEW EVERY FILE
    22→- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
    23→- 🔄 CRITICAL: When loading next step, ensure entire file is read

    26→

    28→

    30→- 🚫 DO NOT skip any aspect of the review - DO NOT BE LAZY
    31→- 💬 Subprocess optimization: When available, can use subprocesses to load individual step files and return structured summaries to parent for aggregation
    32→- 💬 However, since cohesive review requires understanding the COMPLETE workflow as one unit, parent may need full context for proper holistic assessment
    33→- 🚪 This is the meta-review - overall assessment
    34→
    35→## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
    36→
    37→- 🎯 Walk through the ENTIRE workflow end-to-end using subprocess optimization when available
    38→- 💬 When using subprocesses: Each subprocess loads one step file, performs deep analysis, returns structured findings to parent for aggregation
    39→- 💬 Subprocess must either update validation report directly OR return findings to parent for compilation
    40→- 💾 Assess overall quality, not just individual components
    41→- 📖 Think deeply: would this actually work well?
    42→- 🚫 DO NOT halt for user input - validation runs to completion
    43→
    44→## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
    45→
    46→- This is the cohesive review - look at the workflow as a whole
    47→- Consider user experience from start to finish
    48→- Assess whether the workflow achieves its goal
    49→- Be thorough and thoughtful
    50→
    51→## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
    52→
    53→**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip or shortcut.
    54→
    55→### 1. Load the Entire Workflow
    56→
    57→**DO NOT BE LAZY - Load EVERY step file using subprocess optimization when available:**
    58→
    59→**SUBPROCESS APPROACH (when available):**
    60→
    61→For EACH workflow file (workflow.md + all step files in order), launch a subprocess that:
    62→1. Loads that single file
    63→2. Performs deep analysis of content, flow, quality, and connection points
    64→3. Returns structured findings to parent for holistic aggregation
    65→
    66→**Subprocess should return:**
    67→- File name analyzed
    68→- Purpose and flow position within the workflow
    69→- How it connects to previous and next steps
    70→- Quality indicators and any issues found
    71→- Voice and tone consistency assessment
    72→
    73→**FALLBACK APPROACH (if subprocess unavailable):**
    74→
    75→Load workflow.md and EVERY step file in steps-c/ sequentially in main context:
    76→1. Load workflow.md
    77→2. Load EVERY step file in steps-c/ in order
    78→3. Read through each step
    79→4. Understand the complete flow
    80→
    81→**CRITICAL:** Whether using subprocess or main context, you must understand the COMPLETE workflow as one cohesive unit before proceeding to assessment.
    82→
    83→### 2. Walk Through the Workflow Mentally
    84→
    85→**Imagine you are a user running this workflow:**
    86→
    87→- Starting from workflow.md
    88→- Going through step-01
    89→- Progressing through each step
    90→- Experiencing the interactions
    91→- Reaching the end
    92→
    93→**Ask yourself:**
    94→- Does this make sense?
    95→- Is the flow logical?
    96→- Would I feel guided or confused?
    97→- Does it achieve its goal?
    98→
    99→### 3. Assess Cohesiveness
   100→
   101→**Check for:**
   102→
   103→**✅ Cohesive Indicators:**
   104→- Each step builds on previous work
   105→- Clear progression toward goal
   106→- Consistent voice and approach throughout
   107→- User always knows where they are
   108→- Satisfying completion
   109→
   110→**❌ Incohesive Indicators:**
   111→- Steps feel disconnected
   112→- Jumps in logic or flow
   113→- Inconsistent patterns
   114→- User might be confused
   115→- Abrupt or unclear ending
   116→
   117→### 4. Assess Overall Quality
   118→
   119→**Evaluate the workflow across key dimensions:**
   120→
   121→Consider goal clarity, logical flow, facilitation quality, user experience, and goal achievement. Provide an overall quality assessment based on these dimensions.
   122→
   123→### 5. Identify Strengths and Weaknesses
   124→
   125→**Strengths:**
   126→- What does this workflow do well?
   127→- What makes it excellent?
   128→- What should other workflows emulate?
   129→
   130→**Weaknesses:**
   131→- What could be improved?
   132→- What doesn't work well?
   133→- What would confuse users?
   134→
   135→**Critical Issues:**
   136→- Are there any show-stopper problems?
   137→- Would this workflow fail in practice?
   138→
   139→### 6. Provide Recommendation
   140→
   141→**Assess overall workflow readiness:**
   142→
   143→Determine if the workflow is excellent (ready to use, exemplifies best practices), good (solid with minor improvements possible), needs work (has issues to address), or problematic (major issues requiring significant revision). Provide a clear recommendation on readiness for use.
   144→
   145→### 7. Document Findings
   146→
   147→**Document your cohesive review findings in the validation report:**
   148→
   149→Include your overall assessment (excellent/good/needs work/problematic), quality evaluation across key dimensions, cohesiveness analysis (flow, progression, voice and tone), identified strengths and weaknesses, any critical issues, what makes the workflow work well, what could be improved, user experience forecast, and your recommendation on readiness for use.
   150→

   152→
   153→Update {validationReportFile} - replace "## Cohesive Review *Pending...*" with actual findings.
   154→

   157→**CRITICAL:** Save the validation report BEFORE loading next step.
   158→
   159→Then immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}.

   162→"**Cohesive Review complete.** Proceeding to finalize validation report..."

   166→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS

   168→### ✅ SUCCESS:

   171→- Quality assessed across multiple dimensions
   172→- Strengths and weaknesses documented
   173→- Thoughtful recommendation provided
   174→- Findings appended to report
   175→- Report saved before proceeding
   176→- Next validation step loaded
   177→
   178→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   179→
   180→- Not reviewing the entire workflow
   181→- Superficial or lazy assessment
   182→- Not documenting strengths/weaknesses
   183→- Not providing clear recommendation
   184→- Not saving report before proceeding
   185→
   186→**Master Rule:** Validation is systematic and thorough. DO NOT BE LAZY. Review the ENTIRE workflow cohesively. Think deeply about quality. Auto-proceed through all validation steps.

   121→- Guided edits: User specifies what to fix, help create specific edits for user approval
   122→- Edit workflow: If the workflow has steps-e/, use the edit workflow to make systematic changes
   123→
   124→The exact format doesn't matter - just ensure the user understands their options for addressing issues.
   125→
   126→### 8. Update Plan with Validation Status
   127→
   128→If a plan file exists at {workflowPlanFile}, update its frontmatter to include the validation status (COMPLETE), the current validation date, and a reference to the validation report file.
   129→
   130→## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
   131→
   132→This is the final validation step. User reviews findings and decides whether to make changes. Validation workflow ends here.
   133→
   134→---
   135→
   136→## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
   137→
   138→### ✅ SUCCESS:
   139→
   140→- All validation findings summarized
   141→- Complete report presented to user
   142→- Summary section added to report
   143→- Report status updated to COMPLETE
   144→- User can review findings and decide on changes
   145→- Plan updated with validation status
   146→
   147→### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
   148→
   149→- Not summarizing all findings
   150→- Not presenting complete report to user
   151→- Not updating report status
   152→- Not giving user clear options for next steps
   153→
   154→**Master Rule:** Validation is complete. User reviews findings and decides what changes to make. Provide clear summary and options.
   155→
```

index 5e5151e..b6b7a0a 100644

@@ -162,6 +162,17 @@ The `setup-forge` workflow detects available tools and writes the tier to `forge
 
 > **Recommended:** If your IDE supports MCP servers, install the [ast-grep MCP server](https://github.com/ast-grep/ast-grep-mcp) alongside the CLI. SKF's extraction protocol prefers the MCP tool for compact, memory-efficient AST queries and falls back to CLI streaming for large codebases.
 
+### Security Scanning (Optional)
+
+`skill-check` includes security scanning via [Snyk Agent Scan](https://github.com/snyk/snyk-agent-scan) to check for prompt injection risks, sensitive data exposure, and unsafe tool permissions. To enable:
+
+1. Create a free Snyk account at [app.snyk.io](https://app.snyk.io)
+2. Copy your API token from Account Settings
+3. Add to your environment: `export SNYK_TOKEN=your-token`
+4. Re-run `@Ferris SF` to detect the token
+
+Security scanning runs automatically during validation. Use `--no-security-scan` to skip. Security scan availability does not affect your tier level.
+
 ## Knowledge Base
 
 SKF relies on a curated skill compilation knowledge base:
@@ -223,6 +234,7 @@ SKF builds on these excellent open-source tools:
 | [ast-grep MCP](https://github.com/ast-grep/ast-grep-mcp)     | MCP server for memory-efficient AST queries (recommended)          |
 | [QMD](https://github.com/tobi/qmd)                           | Local hybrid search engine for knowledge indexing (Deep tier)      |
 | [skill-check](https://github.com/thedaviddias/skill-check)   | Skill validation, auto-fix, quality scoring, and security scanning |
+| [Snyk Agent Scan](https://github.com/snyk/snyk-agent-scan)   | Security scanning for prompt injection and data exposure (optional) |
